### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.

Alberta independence referendum (19 October)

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026

The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and
investment, infrastructure, technology and broader strategic cooperation. The security dimension will be
particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East
conflicts in exchange for economic cooperation. The summit comes amid at a critical juncture where

Europe is recalibrating its external partnerships.
The Central Committee of the Communist Party of China will hold its Fifth Plenary Session in Beijing.
This will be one of the most important events on the country’s political calendar ahead of the 21st Party
Congress in late 2027.
The Plenum will focus on Party governance, and investors will scrutinise signals regarding leadership
changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

While fifth plenums historically focus on economic planning, the development guidelines for the 15th
Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026
Listed in chronological order
Summit between the EU and the Gulf Cooperation Council (24 October)
Source: istockphoto
Fifth Plenum of China’s Central Committee (26-29 October)

### Page 7

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,
Lebanon and Iran. This is the first Knesset since 1988 to actually complete a full four-year term without
early dissolution. Benjamim Netanyahu remains the dominant single figure but faces a fragmented field:
Naftali Bennett’s new “Together” party is polling as the main rival bloc, alongside Gadi Eisenkot’s Yashar,

Benny Gantz’s Blue and White, and the further right Ben Gvir and Smotrich factions.
Israel’s proportional system means the election is effectively about coalition formation. Key faultlines to
watch: the ultra-Orthodox draft-exemption fight, how the war’s legacy and hostage/reconstruction issues
in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working
majority or whether the Bennett/Eisenkot-style “anyone but Bibi” coalitions gain enough traction. The

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.

It will be a test of whether the G20 can sustain cooperation amid intensifying geopolitical fragmentation.
In addition, Russia has confirmed its intent to actively participate in the meeting. This would further
normalize Russia’s multilateral engagement. For Europe and other partners, a key question will be the
extent to which the G20 can be a forum for broader international cooperation.

Document 410: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.

FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
Fiji’s resilience is being tested...
Fiji’s economy entered 2026 with growth already normalising
from the post-pandemic recovery. Real GDP growth slowed from
3.6% in 2024 to 2.5% in 2025. Foreign reserves started the year

around $3.7bn, banking-system liquidity remained high and the
RBF maintained its Overnight Policy Rate at 0.25%.
Activity so far into 2026 has been somewhat stronger than what
we feared. Visitor arrivals reached 762,352 during January–
September, up 3.7%yr, while construction, investment lending,
cement sales, remittances and private-sector credit remained
firm. Nevertheless, Westpac Fiji has kept its 2026 growth
forecast unchanged at 2.0%. Stronger headline indicators are

not translating one-for-one into real economic growth because
higher prices and intermediate costs are absorbing a growing
share of nominal activity.
The outlook changed materially in March as the Middle East
conflict disrupted global energy markets. Fiji remains heavily
dependent on imported petroleum products, so higher refined-
fuel, freight and insurance costs flowed through transport,
electricity, food distribution and wider business expenses. By

June, motor spirit price reached $3.93/litre and diesel at a record
$4.58/litre, while automotive-fuel sales value rose 24.4%yr in the
June quarter.
As this report goes out, the Middle East conflict remains a
key external downside risk. At least 12 vessels were attacked
in the week ending 16 October and yet it is reported that oil
flows through Hormuz averaged 11.6mn barrels per day to 13
October, about 68% of pre-war levels through alternative and

costly routes. Iran says a seven-day reopening proposal remains
available, but US-Iran negotiations remain unresolved.
Westpac Economics’ most recent scenario analysis “ Persistent
energy pressures have further to run ” indicates that relief will
be gradual. Brent crude is forecast to average US$101/bbl in Q4
2026 before easing through 2027. Refined products are expected
to normalise more slowly because refinery capacity, inventories,
freight and insurance remain constrained. Under Westpac’s

escalation scenario, in a return to open conflict Brent could reach
around US$150/bbl in H1 2027.
Domestic tourism sector demonstrates the nominal-versus-
real growth tension. June-quarter visitor arrivals increased to
287,588, yet earnings fell 4.9%yr to $708.1mn. First-half tourism
earnings declined 1.7%yr despite higher arrivals, even though
hotel takings increased 9.5%yr.
Australia and New Zealand remain critical, together contributing
around 71% of September arrivals. Westpac expects Australian

growth to slow from 2.1% in 2026 to 1.5% in 2027, while New
Zealand strengthens from 1.9% to 2.6%. Tourism therefore
increasingly needs to focus on yield rather than visitor numbers
alone.
Shamal Chand
Senior Economist, Westpac Pacific Economics

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
...but the buffers are still doing the heavy lifting...
There are good reasons why growth held up despite these shocks.
Construction work put in place reached $155.9mn in the June
quarter, up 8.6%yr, while investment lending, cement sales and

construction-related imports subsequently remained strong.
Industrial production increased 1.5%yr, although performance
remained uneven across industries.
Inward personal remittances have been particularly impressive,
reaching $1.2bn during January–August, up 32.7%yr. We expect
remittances to reach $1.55bn this year. These inflows, with strong
diaspora support, are helping households absorb higher living
costs while simultaneously supporting Fiji’s external position.

However, the external accounts show the cost of this resilience.
Fiji’s merchandise trade deficit widened to $2.9bn in the first half,
with imports reaching $4.3bn against exports of $1.4bn. Mineral-
fuel imports alone reached around $1.2bn, up 57%yr. Foreign
reserves nevertheless recovered to approximately $4.09bn by
end-September, equivalent to 5.7 months of retained imports , also
supported by Government’s external loan draw-down.
Our in-house import-demand indicator is signalling softer

domestic momentum. Import activity over the three months
to September was down 21.3%yr and fell 39.8% compared
with the preceding three months. The broader six-month
measure declined at a more moderate 16.1% annualised pace.
While volatile, the trend adds to our view that higher prices are
weighing on real activity, despite continued strength across
tourism, remittances, credit and construction.
Headline inflation reached 7.6%yr in August before easing to

6.8% in September, with transport prices up 12.2%yr and food
prices up 7.6%. Our latest forecast has November inflation
around 8.0%yr, average inflation around 4.3% for 2026 and
December (year-end) inflation at 7.4%.
Higher fuel prices are no longer affecting only households.
The shock is moving through freight, electricity, agriculture,
construction materials and wider supply chains. For instance,
we note recent increases in taxi and bus fares. These higher

intermediate costs compress business margins and reduce
household purchasing power.
This also informs our 2027 view. Westpac Fiji has downgraded
its 2027 growth forecast to 2.5%, reflecting the prevailing
effects of the energy crisis, elevated refined-fuel prices and the
broader transmission of higher intermediate costs into next year.
Westpac’s global outlook supports this caution: crude prices are
expected to ease gradually, but refined products could remain

comparatively expensive for longer.
Renewable energy is increasingly important because the fuel
crisis has put a clear economic price on Fiji’s imported-energy
dependence. The faster Fiji prioritises a shift to renewable energy,
the sooner it can establish a more stable energy foundation.
El Niño creates an additional risk through agriculture, hydro
generation and food prices. The buffers remain meaningful, but
they are being asked to work significantly harder.

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
...as risks converge
Government finances add another dimension to the outlook.
The FY2025/26 deficit came in at $597.9mn, or 4.3% of GDP,
substantially better than originally budgeted.
Nevertheless, financing requirements are substantial.

Government plans to raise $1.48bn in FY2026/27, comprising
$1.046bn in deficit financing and $436.3mn refinancing. Around
$853.9mn to be domestically sourced and $627.95mn externally,
including a $444mn ADB policy-based loan in November.
Domestic yields therefore deserve closer attention. Fiji’s
government yield curve has steepened considerably, with the
adjustment concentrated beyond 10 years. While the 10-year
yield remained at 3.90%, the 20-year yield rose by 80 basis points

between July 2025 and September 2026 to 5.80%. The 25-year
yield increased even more sharply, climbing from 5.94% to 7.19%
after peaking at 7.25% in mid-2026.
Pressure is also emerging at the short end. The Government’s
20 October Treasury Bill tender offered $50mn across three-,
six- and 12-month maturities. Investors bid for the full amount,
but sought yields of 0.35%, 0.60% and 1.30%, respectively,
above the listed yields of 0.30%, 0.55% and 1.25%. Government

did not accept the higher yields, leaving the entire $50mn offer
unallocated. This suggests investors are increasingly seeking
better returns even on shorter-dated Government securities.
The financial system still provides considerable protection.
Banking-system liquidity stood around $2.28bn at end-
September and the RBF maintained its OPR at 0.25%. In August,
the weighted-average lending rate was 4.51%, the savings-
deposit rate 0.31% and the time-deposit rate 2.01%.

For now, high liquidity limits immediate crowding-out risks.
Higher government yields may encourage banks and institutional
investors to allocate more funds towards government securities.
If liquidity subsequently tightens, deposit rates may need to rise
to retain savings, adding pressure to bank funding costs and
eventually lending rates.
S&P’s decision to maintain Fiji’s B+/B ratings but revise the
outlook from stable to negative reinforces the importance of

debt sustainability, investor confidence and credible medium-
term fiscal consolidation.
Political uncertainty adds another layer, with ongoing public
debate on constitutional amendments and the general election
due by 6 February 2027.
This brings us back to the growth story. We have maintained
2026 growth at 2.0% because resilient tourism, remittances,
construction and investment are being offset by unexpectedly
high inflation and intermediate costs. Our downgrade of 2027

growth to 2.5% recognises that this adjustment will take longer
than previously expected, while fiscal, financial, energy, climate
and political risks increasingly overlap.

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
INFLATION OUTLOOK
Cost pressures move beyond the pump
Inflation has emerged as a key pressure point for Fiji, threatening 
to erode gains from otherwise resilient domestic activity. 
Headline inflation accelerated to 6.8%yr in September, driven by 
essentials: food prices rose 7.6%yr, transport increased 12.2%yr 
and housing and utilities were 4.9% higher. The underlying 
picture remains less severe, with prices declining 0.4%mth in 
September and annual-average inflation at 1.9%. Nevertheless, 
the acceleration suggests the global fuel shock is increasingly 
working its way through the domestic economy.

The import-price pipeline reinforces this view. Import prices 
increased 4.9%qtr and 4.4%yr in the June quarter, including 
a 15.4%yr rise in mineral-product prices as petrol, diesel and 
aviation-fuel costs increased. Export prices rose 6.2%yr, although 
the terms of trade eased 0.6%qtr. Elevated energy costs are 
also spreading beyond the pump. Transport-services producer 
prices increased 2.7%qtr in June, while building-material prices 
rose 1.5%qtr in September, signalling broader pressure across 
distribution, construction and services.

Fuel-price pressures intensified again in October, when regulated 
unleaded prices in Viti Levu increased to $3.15/litre and diesel 
rose by 21 cents to $3.41/litre. Prices nevertheless remain below 
their June peaks. Our indicative estimates point to further 
increases in fuel prices over the coming months.

The global outlook offers little immediate relief. Westpac 
Economics expects Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 2026 
before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027, but refined fuels are 
expected to ease more slowly as refinery supply remains more 
constrained, while shipping, insurance and inventory pressures 
gradually normalise. Westpac’s Australian forecasts provide 
some regional context, with diesel and petrol expected to 
average 274c/l and 230c/l, respectively, in Q4 before declining 
through 2027.

For Fiji, the distinction between crude and refined-product prices 
is important. Singapore gasoil prices, freight and insurance costs 
are expected to remain elevated and ease only gradually through 
2027, meaning lower Brent prices may not translate immediately 
into cheaper imported fuel. Higher diesel costs feed into freight, 
construction, agriculture and distribution, while aviation-
fuel pressures add to airline operating costs and tourism-
sector margins. Repeated shocks increase the likelihood that 
businesses eventually pass more of these costs on to consumers, 
particularly through food and other essentials.

We consequently expect inflation to remain elevated through 
year-end, rising towards 8.0% in November before easing to 7.4% 
in December, taking annual-average inflation to around 4.3%. 
The concern is increasingly that an imported fuel shock develops 
into broader cost-push inflation into next year. The squeeze on 
real purchasing power makes inflation an increasingly important 
downside risk to Fiji’s growth outlook heading into 2027.
Source: FBoS, Macrobond, Westpac Economics

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
CREDIT RATING OUTLOOK
S&P negative outlook is concerning
Fiji’s sovereign credit profile has come under renewed pressure
recently, adding another layer of caution to an economy already
navigating softer real growth and elevated external risks.

S&P maintained Fiji’s long-term rating at B+ and short-term
rating at B but revised the outlook from Stable to Negative.
While not a downgrade, it signals increased risk if fiscal
and debt metrics deteriorate. Historically, four of Fiji’s six
negative outlooks since 2006 have resulted in downgrades,
typically during periods of major economic or fiscal stress.
Similar vulnerabilities are resurfacing, with rising government
expenditure and guarantees, alongside Middle East tensions and

inflationary pressures.
The key concern is fiscal. S&P viewed the 2026/27 Budget as
more expansionary than previously expected, effectively slowing
the fiscal consolidation achieved in previous years. It projects
the fiscal deficit to average 4.6% of GDP over 2027–29, including
4.9% in FY2027, with net government debt moving above 70% of
GDP. Once contingent liabilities are incorporated, the broader
public-sector exposure approaches 80% of GDP. This leaves

considerably less fiscal space to absorb another major domestic
or external shock.
In S&P’s latest assessment, contingent liabilities are becoming
increasingly important to the sovereign risk story. Government
exposure through guarantees and other contingent liabilities
adds to the broader fiscal risk, while a widening current account
deficit, elevated imports and rising external debt reinforce the
risks surrounding the outlook. For Fiji, this matters because

the next phase of growth will require substantial investment in
infrastructure, climate resilience and productive capacity.
The negative outlook therefore strengthens the case for a more
credible medium-term fiscal consolidation path by the Fiji
Government. S&P indicated that the outlook could return to
Stable if economic growth and tourism outperform its forecasts,
resulting in narrower fiscal deficits and lower debt ratios, or if
improvements in institutional and policy settings sustainably

strengthen fiscal outcomes and debt levels.
Importantly, the assessment across rating agencies is not
uniformly negative. Moody’s maintained Fiji’s B1 long-term issuer
rating with a Stable outlook, suggesting that despite significant
structural and fiscal challenges, the overall credit profile remains
broadly unchanged and risks remain balanced.
A future downgrade would likely reinforce perceptions of
higher sovereign risk just as Fiji requires greater private and

foreign investment. Maintaining fiscal credibility, containing
contingent liabilities and ensuring that new borrowing supports
productivity-enhancing investment will be increasingly
important. With elevated uncertainty, climate vulnerability and
external shocks remaining additional downside risks, the room
for further fiscal slippage is becoming increasingly limited for the
Fijian Government.

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
SUPER EL NIÑO
The threat of a “very strong” El Niño
It is the year of the ‘very strong’ El Niño, and for Fiji the risk is no
longer just on the horizon. Dry conditions are already affecting
parts of the country, while the Government has stepped up

preparations for a potentially more difficult period ahead.
History suggests this matters for the broader economy because
prolonged drought can simultaneously affect agriculture, water
supply and electricity generation before flowing through food
prices, imports, rural incomes and ultimately growth.
Our rainfall analysis covering 1981–2025 shows that rainfall
during El Niño periods was 24.7% lower than under neutral
conditions nationally, with all four regions recording historical

deficits above 20%.
During the 1982–83 El Niño, severe drought coincided with
Cyclone Oscar and a weak global economy. Historical estimates
highlight that Fiji’s economy contracted by 4.0%, against a
pre-disaster forecast of 2.7% growth during the super El Niño
period then. The 1997–98 episode was more severe. By 1998 the
cane crop was forecast at just 1.88mn tonnes, producing around
150,000 tonnes of sugar. Historical estimates used here indicate

cane production fell around 26%, contributing to an aggregate
GDP contraction of at least 1.3%. Fiji also devalued the dollar by
20% in January 1998 amid the overlapping Asian Financial Crisis.
The social cost was significant. In 1998, around 27,562
households needed food relief and 47,592 were already receiving
emergency water supplies according to some surveys. The 2015–
16 episode again brought crop losses and drying water supplies.
The effects are beginning to show

The current event is already leaving a footprint. September
rainfall was drier than usual across the Western, Central and
Eastern divisions and most of the Northern Division, while several
Western Division stations recorded less than 100mm.
More recent monitoring points to serious or very dry conditions
across parts of Vanua Levu, Taveuni, Lomaiviti, Yasawa, Lau and
other areas. Local effects are becoming tangible. Taveuni had
already required assistance with water carting in September,

while NDRMO reported that Fiji was experiencing meteorological
drought and warned it could progress towards hydrological and
agricultural drought if conditions continued.
The Government’s response has consequently shifted towards
preparedness. The National Disaster Risk Management Council
is reviewing the National El Niño Preparedness and Response
Plan. Humanitarian clusters were also instructed to prepare
contingency plans for consolidation and presentation to Cabinet,

while NDRMO has been working with Energy Fiji Limited and the
Water Authority of Fiji to assess emerging risks.
This marks a progression from earlier in the year. In August,
our assessment was that the FY2026–27 Budget contained
no explicit El Niño preparedness measure, implying much
of the initial response would need to occur through existing
institutional arrangements.
The economic transmission remains important. Agriculture feels
reduced rainfall first through weaker soil moisture and water

availability. Lower domestic food production can then lift prices
and import requirements. Electricity provides another channel
as weaker hydro generation could require increased thermal
generation and imported fuel, adding to higher production
costs and foreign-exchange outflows. And Fiji has less room for
another supply-side shock.
Fiji is more diversified than during the droughts of the 1980s
and 1990s, while its reserve position provides an important

buffer. But financial buffers cannot replace rainfall, agricultural
production or water supplies.

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
9
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
RESEARCH INSIGHTS
Why is ‘foreign reserves’ so important for Fiji?
Foreign reserves remain one of the most closely watched indica -
tors of Fiji’s economic health. At the end of September, reserves 
stood at around $4.1 billion, equivalent to 5.7 months of retained 
imports. The figure features prominently in Reserve Bank report -
ing, and for good reason. 
For a small, import-dependent economy operating a managed 
exchange-rate regime, reserves provide an important buffer 
against external shocks.
In simple terms, foreign reserves can be thought of as Fiji’s 
foreign-currency savings account. Fiji earns foreign exchange 
through tourism, remittances, exports, foreign investment and 
external borrowing. At the same time, foreign currency is required 
to pay for imports, service external debt and meet other overseas 
obligations. When foreign-currency inflows exceed outflows, 
reserves generally rise. When the reverse occurs, reserves come 
under pressure.

The analogy is not perfect, but it highlights why reserves matter. 
Unlike some larger economies, Fiji cannot simply rely on its ex -
change rate to absorb external shocks. The Fiji dollar is managed 
against a basket comprising the Australian, US and New Zealand 
dollars, the euro and the Japanese yen. This provides a degree 
of exchange-rate stability and predictability for households and 
businesses, but it also means that foreign reserves are needed to 
effectively provide a shock absorber for the economy.

There is also an important connection with domestic financial 
conditions. Our analysis suggests that before July 2020, every 
$1 million increase in reserves was associated with around $0.31 
million in additional banking-system liquidity. Since then, the 
relationship has strengthened substantially to around $0.83 mil -
lion. Following the pandemic, external borrowing, grants, remit -
tances and the recovery in tourism helped lift foreign-exchange 
inflows and thus banking liquidity, while government expenditure 
and limited sterilisation further contributed to abundant domes -
tic liquidity.

This highlights an important policy trade-off. Economists refer to 
the “impossible trinity”, where an economy cannot simultaneous -
ly maintain a fixed or tightly managed exchange rate, completely 
free capital movement and fully independent monetary policy. 
Fiji’s framework therefore relies on a combination of foreign re -
serves, monetary-policy settings, exchange controls and broader 
market management to maintain stability.

Those controls, however, are not without cost. Restrictions on 
moving capital offshore limit the flexibility available to house -
holds, foreign investors and Fiji-based businesses. 
A deeper question is therefore whether Fiji could eventually move 
towards a more flexible exchange-rate framework.

Probably, but not yet. Greater exchange-rate flexibility could 
theoretically allow the currency itself to absorb more of an 
external shock. But that requires considerably deeper financial 
markets than what Fiji presently has. Foreign-exchange trading 
remains relatively shallow, secondary-market activity in govern -
ment securities is limited, while instruments that allow business -
es to hedge currency risk are significantly underdeveloped.

There is also an important transmission issue. A weaker Fiji dollar 
could theoretically improve export competitiveness, but the 
benefits would take time. Merchandise exports remain con -
strained by domestic production capacity, while tourism is also 
influenced by available capacity and advance bookings. Higher 
import prices, by contrast, would flow through much faster. Given 
Fiji’s heavy reliance on imported fuel, food, machinery and other 
goods, currency depreciation would quickly add to inflation and 
business costs.

The experiences of Australia and Singapore offer two useful per -
spectives.
 Australia floated its dollar in 1983 alongside broader finan -
cial-sector liberalisation and market development. Singapore 
followed a different path. Despite possessing a highly developed 
financial system, it continues to manage its currency against a 
basket, with the exchange rate occupying a central role in mone -
tary policy.

For Fiji, the lesson is not that one exchange-rate regime is nec -
essarily superior to another. Rather, greater flexibility requires 
deeper markets and stronger transmission mechanisms. Devel -
oping the foreign-exchange market, domestic capital markets 
and currency-risk management instruments therefore needs to 
precede any significant shift in the present regime.

Until then, foreign reserves will remain much more than another 
number in the RBF’s monthly statistics. It acts as a buffer against 
external shocks, help underpin confidence in the Fiji dollar and 
provide the economy with room to continue paying for essential 
imports when foreign-currency inflows weaken. At $4.1 billion, 
the reserve position remains comfortable, but maintaining that 
buffer remains fundamental to Fiji’s macroeconomic stability .

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained  
S&P500 volatility index (VIX); US bond volatility index (MOVE)*   10-year government bond yield spreads over German bonds

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *measures US government bond volatility

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary:  Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility . 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation  slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology -sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech -led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds.  Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P 500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near -term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains:  Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock -bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small -caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near -term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity -bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P 500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30 .6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory -chip 
profits and higher long -term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near -term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid -term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&A s. However, a nother 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields , without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a  headwind for equities.  Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near -term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities.  
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid -
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of a n ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the market implications of France's political and 
fiscal uncertainty?   
Our view: France’s political and fiscal uncertainty remain near-
term headwinds for European assets. We are placing our three-
month EUR/USD forecast (1.15) under review for a potential 
downward adjustment, while retaining our 12-month forecast at 
1.18 as broader Euro  area spillovers remain limited. Our base 
case remains one more ECB hike in December followed by a 
prolonged pause, although tighter financial conditions increase 
the risk of a delay. For equities, we retain Euro area banks as 
an Opportunistic idea, supported by strong profitability, capital 
buffers and reasonable valuations. For bank bonds, we prefer a 
subordinated capital structure to senior debt. 
Rationale: France’s 10-year government bond yield has risen amid 
renewed political and fiscal concerns. This has tightened financial 
conditions and added to the near-term EUR risk premium. Together 
with elevated US yields, this raises downside risks to our three-
month EUR/USD view. However, the stress has not yet developed 
into broad Euro area contagion, and recent expectations of progress 
on France’s 2027 budget have provided some relief. Broader Euro  
area growth and labour  market fundamentals also remain relatively 
resilient, supporting our unchanged 12-month EUR/USD view. 
For the ECB, higher sovereign yields are already delivering some 
monetary tightening. Markets have consequently pared expectations 
of additional ECB hikes. We continue to expect one more hike in 
December, given elevated near -term inflation, but persistent fiscal 
or political stress in France and Spain could delay further tightening. 
For Euro area banks, wider sovereign spreads  are likely to  raise 
funding costs and pressure bond portfolios, while fewer ECB hikes 
would moderate further gains in net interest income. Nevertheless, 
profitability remains strong, capital buffers are healthy and price-
to-book valuations remain reasonable, with returns on equity also 
improving. We, therefore, maintain Euro area banks as an 
Opportunistic idea , while monitoring sovereign spreads, funding 
costs and any deterioration in loan-loss provisions.  
On the bond side, European bank junior subordinated bond prices 
fell by an average of 3-5 cash points before rebounding, with French 
bank subordinated bonds underperforming those of other European 
peers. As fundamentals remain strong, we view pullbacks as 
opportunities to add and maintain our preference for a subordinated 
capital structure over senior debt for European bank bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist  
 Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the likelihood of elevated long -end government 
bond yields evolving into a systemic risk for the financial sector 
and public finances? 
Our view: We believe s ystemic risk remains a tail risk rather 
than a base case in the near term. The probability would rise 
materially if rollover stress, foreign demand withdrawal and 
private credit deterioration occur in combination. 
Rationale: Higher bond yields worsen fiscal deficits and drive higher 
issuance requirements, exerting further upward pressure on yields. 
The resulting tighter financial conditions weigh on economic growth. 
This feedback loop is most acute in Developed Markets (DMs) where 
debt burdens are already elevated. For instance, projected 2026 
debt-to-GDP ratios stand at 204% for Japan, 138% for Italy, 126% 
for the US, 118% for France and 104% for the UK. Countries with 
shorter average debt maturities face the sharpest near-term 
repricing risk  as maturing obligations are refinanced at current 
rates. Meanwhile, evidence suggests that  a positive equity-bond 
correlation increases the sensitivity of yields to bond supply.  
Taking the US as an example, there has been no buyers’ strike on 
US government bonds yet , though foreign demand has softened, 
narrowing the margin of safety. The US government has shifted 
towards shorter-dated issuance to manage funding costs (reducing 
near-term expenses, but concentrating rollover risk). Economic 
resilience remains evident, underpinned by corporate confidence 
in AI -driven investment . The key question is whether this 
expansion can persist long enough to stabilise the fiscal trajectory. 
Within the financial sector, corporate balance sheets  remain 
strong, while High-Yield (HY) bond and leveraged loan default rates 
remain contained, though this reflects the lag between rate rises and 
credit deterioration, not immunity. Following the failure of the Silicon 
Valley Bank, liquidity discipline among banks has improved, but 
duration mismatches persist.  
In private credit, default rates are trending upward while the asset 
class has come under continued scrutiny , with negative headlines 
and a rise in redemptions trigger ing fund gating  at some  private 
credit managers since Q 1 2026. Hence, continued monitoring of 
default rates, redemptions and potential stress events in private 
credit remains essential . That said, higher rates could buoy 
medium-term returns and distribution yields in the asset class , 
as the majority of direct lending loans have a floating-rate structure. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the US Q3 2026 earnings season, 
especially for the banking sector? 
Our view: We expect another quarter of robust US earnings 
growth to support our Overweight stance on US equities. We 
see m ajor US banks, which begin reporting next week, also 
delivering healthy results, reinforcing our Overweight view on  
the US financial sector. 
Rationale: Consensus forecasts S&P500 Q3 earnings growth  of 
30.6% y/y, up from 27.6% at the start of July. Energy is expected to 
post the strongest growth at 114.7%, supported by elevated oil 
prices amid ongoing geopolitical tensions. Information technology 
(66.5%) and communication services (49.0%) remain key 
earnings drivers, benefiting from sustained AI -related demand and 
investment. The broader outlook remains supportive, with full-year 
earnings growth projected at 35.8% in 2026 and 15.6% in 2027. 
For banks, the recent underperformance amid concerns over higher 
long-term bond yields has, in our view, improved the forward-looking 
risk-reward profile.  Over the medium term, strengthening loan 
growth and resilient credit quality should continue to support solid 
fundamentals. Capital markets activity also remains constructive, 
with industry leaders well -positioned to benefit from their strong 
investment banking and trading franchises. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  How could upcoming semiconductor manufacturing and 
equipment earnings reshape the AI capex outlook? 
Our view: We maintain our AI capex outlook as robust demand 
and ongoing supply constraints support a multi-year expansion 
cycle. In the near term, however, we take a more prudent stance 
on semiconductors amid an expected rise in equity volatility. 
Rationale: Recent memory industry guidance points to sustained 
investment in advanced chip manufacturing , with customer 
commitments extending through 2031. Upcoming earnings should 
provide visibility into capacity expansion plans. This supports our AI 
capex outlook, which we will monitor through the earnings season.  
That said, we expect equity volatility to rise in the near term amid 
elevated long-term yields and macroeconomic uncertainty. Recent 
media reports on AI lab revenues  have also led to doubts over 
monetisation and the ability to sustain elevated capex spending. We 
thus took profit on our Global Semiconductors (8.1% return from 9-
Sep-26 to 2-Oct-26) and MSCI Taiwan (10.0% return from 9-Jul-26 to 
2-Oct-26) Opportunistic ideas following strong performance. This does 
not reflect a deterioration in fundamentals, but rather a need to be 
prudent on high-beta exposure. We remain constructive on the AI 
theme over the long term. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  USD/CHF has rebounded sharply over the past two 
months. What are the key factors behind the move, and do you 
see the currency pair continuing to move higher? 
Our view:  USD/CHF tested 0.84 amid strong momentum from 
the USD, but we see the pair remain ing supported near -term 
before gradually moving lower towards 0.82 in three months. 
Rationale: The Fed’s relatively high policy rate continues to support 
USD carry demand , while the Swiss National Bank maintains its 
policy rate at 0%. In the near term, however, the Swiss franc (CHF) 
could benefit from increased safe-haven demand amid mounting 
fiscal concerns in France  and political uncertainty in Spain , 
particularly if European risk premia continue to rise. Beyond these 
cyclical factors, Switzerland’s substantial net foreign asset position, 
persistent current account surpluses and low public debt burden 
continue to underpin the CHF’s structural strength . Over the 
medium term, we expect the USD’s rate advantage to gradually 
diminish as the Fed approaches the end of its tightening cycle, while 
the CHF’s defensive characteristics should remain supportive.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Do you see further upside for USD/JPY after recent dovish 
comments from BoJ officials and latest bond auctions? 
Our view: We see some near-term upside risk to USD/JPY from 
elevated US  yields, but retain our medium -term constructive 
JPY view. USD/JPY remains below 159, suggesting markets are 
mindful of intervention risk as the pair approaches 160. We 
continue to expect further BoJ normalisation. 
Rationale: Recent BoJ communication appears less dovish than the 
market narrative had suggested. BoJ Governor Ueda continues to 
emphasise the need to anchor underlying inflation around 2%  
and gradually reduce monetary accommodation, supporting our view 
that the tightening cycle is not yet over. An October pause would 
reflect a more measured pace of policy normalisation  rather than a 
change in the BoJ’s policy direction. 
Recent Japanese government bond (JGB) auctions have shown 
resilient demand despite higher yields.  At the latest 10 -year JGB 
auction, the bid -to-cover ratio improved  to 3.76x from 3.29x 
previously, while the auction tail (spread between highest 
accepted yield and issued yield) narrowed to 0.2bps from 1.6bps. 
We see this as indicative of an orderly, functioning market rather than 
a dovish BoJ signal . Intervention concerns around  the 160 level 
should help limit near-term USD/JPY upside.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

Document 451: “这种模式我们见过！”大空头交易员详解：为何做空AI新贵CoreWeave

Source PDF: all report pdf/“这种模式我们见过！”大空头交易员详解：为何做空AI新贵CoreWeave.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

《⼤空头》三位原型交易员创⽴的 Seawolf Capital 已建⽴ CoreWeave 空头头⼨，认为其
⾼资本消耗、 GPU 经济寿命不确定及竞争性芯⽚供给扩张，令商业模式⾯临⻛险。更关键
的是， CoreWeave 未来需要持续融资数百亿美元，若融资成本上升或资本获取受阻，业务
将难以为继。这笔做空并⾮看空 AI ⾏业，⽽是押注其融资能⼒与资本成本。
摘要：
因《⼤空头》⼀书和电影⽽声名⼤噪的交易员们，正将⽬光瞄准 AI 算⼒租赁公司 CoreWeave 。
由三位 " ⼤空头 " 交易员联合创⽴的投资机构 Seawolf Capital 公开宣布做空 CoreWeave ，并系统
阐述了其看空逻辑 —— 这家公司的商业模式似曾相识，⽽资本获取能⼒才是真正的致命软肋。
Seawolf 联合创始⼈ Vincent Daniel 在⼀篇 Substack ⽂章中披露，该机构已建⽴ CoreWeave 空头
头⼨，并将其商业模式与多家资产租赁公司进⾏类⽐。他指出， GPU 的经济寿命可能远不及⽯

油钻井平台、⻜机或航运设备，⽽竞争性芯⽚供给正在以惊⼈速度扩张，这使 CoreWeave 的护
城河存在根本性疑问。
Daniel 强调，这⼀空头押注的核⼼逻辑并⾮看空 AI ⾏业整体前景，⽽是对资本可得性与融资成本
的判断。他认为，科技公司正与政府争夺融资资源，叠加货币政策收紧周期，CoreWeave 若⽆
法以低廉成本持续融资，其商业模式将难以为继。 CoreWeave 股价近期已因债务问题引发市场
担忧⽽出现明显下跌。
从⼀条社交媒体帖⼦到系统性做空
Seawolf 的 CoreWeave 空头头⼨，起源于⼀个颇具戏剧性的契机。
Daniel 表⽰，此前有⼈在 X 平台上嘲讽那些做空 CoreWeave 却缺乏财务分析⽀撑的投资者，这反
⽽促使 Seawolf 团队决定认真研究这家公司。
" 很难反驳这种说法，所以我们决定认真研究⼀番， "Daniel 写道。 "当我们深⼊挖掘 CoreWeave
的故事时，开始意识到它的商业模式看起来⾮常熟悉。 "
Daniel 与 Seawolf 联合创始⼈ Danny Moses 、 Porter Collins 共同对 CoreWeave 展开深度研究。

CoreWeave 的核⼼业务是租赁以英伟达 GPU 为主的⾼性能 AI 算⼒，属于所谓 " 新云 " （ neocloud ）
赛道。
" 这种模式我们⻅过 " ：与传统资产租赁公司的类⽐
Seawolf 将 CoreWeave 的商业模式与多家传统资产租赁公司进⾏了对⽐，包括向⽯油公司租赁钻
井平台的 Transocean ，以及航空设备租赁公司 AerCap Holdings 。
值得注意的是，Seawolf 同时持有上述两家公司的头⼨，但明确表⽰更倾向于这两家，⽽⾮
CoreWeave 。
原因在于资产的经济寿命与供给约束存在本质差异。 Daniel 指出， Seawolf 偏好租赁那些底层供
给受到结构性约束、远期订单规模有限的资产。 " 尽管算⼒需求⽆疑⼗分旺盛，但竞争性芯⽚产
能的远期供给正在以惊⼈速度扩张。 " 相⽐之下， GPU 的经济寿命存在更⼤的不确定性。
资本结构是核⼼⻛险
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

" 基于预计 2027 年的⾃由现⾦流缺⼝以及当前资产负债表的杠杆⽔平（具体⽽⾔是有形普通股权

与 WeWork 的类⽐，以及对 AI ⼤局的判断

Playbook" 节⽬中与另⼀位 " ⼤空头 " 投资者 Steve Eisman 同台，将 CoreWeave ⽐作 WeWork——
这家曾经的硅⾕办公空间独⻆兽如今已成为科技创业公司的警⽰案例。
不过， Daniel 明确区分了这⼀空头押注与对 AI ⾏业整体的判断。他表⽰， Seawolf 的 CoreWeave

是我对 CoreWeave 所做的判断， "Daniel 对 Business Insider 表⽰。

1
《銀行獲利穩增長，利收動能接棒領航，資本市場
短暫休整》
大型銀行 3Q26獲利成長延續 ，但幅度收斂 ，主要
是非利息收入表現開始降溫，多數銀行認為投行案
源充足，需求只是遞延而非消失，債權相關業績因
利率走高而下滑，但股權相關業績仍有收獲；相比
之下，3Q獲利貢獻焦點一致轉向了淨利息收入 ，
業績增長得益於貸款與存款穩定成長，而財富管理
業務也提供了顯著的成長。部份銀行非利息費用成
長幅度大於收入成長幅度，導致效率比率變差。儘
管信貸品質仍健康無虞，但仍有部份銀行對潛在風
險展現謹慎態度，因而提高壞帳準備金。

展望後市，因淨利息收入的前景相對清晰，故可見
美大型銀行因此維持或上調 2026全年淨利息收入
預期，或上修 2026年股東權益報酬率 ，並重申或
下修壞帳率。相比之下，資本市場業務則需觀望，

《摩根大通：企業投資與消費穩健 ，高利率紅利延
續》
3Q26營收年增8%、每股盈餘年增 10%。淨利息收
入年增13%，受存款、信用卡及企業貸款成長帶動；
非利息收入年增3%；市場收入年增15%，由股票交
易大增帶動 ，固收業務下滑；投行業務年增 15%，
主要受到諮詢費收入成長所帶動；非利息費用支出
年增12%，效率比率變差 ，呆帳與準備金皆較去年

同期減少。公司上調2026年淨利息收入預估，並預
期2027年進一步成長，反映資產負債表擴張及高利
率環境利多 。管理層認為美國消費與企業投資仍具
韌性，惟通膨、升息、財政赤字及地緣政治風險仍
是後續觀察重點。
《花旗：上調2026利收預期、下調壞帳率、對潛在
風險謹慎》
3Q26營收年增8%、每股盈餘年增 53%。淨利息收
入成長14%，受惠服務、財富管理 、市場及信用卡
業務擴張；非利息收入下滑4%；市場收入受益於股
票收入成長；投資銀行收入受益於股權資本市場 ，
部份被併購諮詢與債權資本市場下滑所抵消；公司
增加壞帳準備金反映了管理潛在經濟風險的謹慎態
度；公司預期第四季市場收入將出現典型的季節性
下滑。公司上調2026年淨利息收入與股東權益報酬
率預期，下修壞帳率 。管理層看好美國消費與企業
活動韌性 ，認為高利率環境及 AI投資需求可持續支
撐成長。

Document 581: 凱基投顧_DECK Deckers_劉昃恩_20261027

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_DECK Deckers_劉昃恩_20261027.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

十月 27, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  運動 用品  ‧美國

Deckers 
(DECK.O/DECK US)

美國市場疲態顯現 
重要訊息  
Deckers  2Q FY27 (財年止於 3 月；7-9 月) 財報 優於預期 ，但展望 暴露美國
消費前景趨 疲。管理層下調 FY27 營收展望 ，並表示市場庫存調整可能延伸
至 2027 年春夏季。  
評論及分析  
2Q FY27 利潤率為亮點 。2Q FY27 稅後淨利 年增 17%至 3.1 億美元，折合
每股盈餘 2.32 美元，優於市場預期 28%，主因利潤率超前， 且有美國政府
退稅 挹注每股盈餘 0.28 美元。營收年增 4%至 14.8 億美元 ，略低於指引，
係受歐洲第三方物流  (3PL) 轉型導致運輸延誤及部分電商業務疲軟影響。
排除一次性退稅利益，全價銷售、 產品組合優化及 費用控管帶動毛利率及
營利率分別年增、季增至 57.4% 、23.2% ，高於市場預估 。 
美國市場 壓力 漸增 。管理層觀察到美國消費者採購行為轉變，客流量集中
促銷活動期間， 並有延後購買現 象。加上運動鞋領域同業促銷競爭加劇，
公司 堅守價格底線 導致直接面對消費者銷售成長明顯趨緩， 2Q FY27 美國
銷售 再度陷入年減 0.4%。作為應對策略，管理層計畫強化 高端產品在歐美
批發分銷管道布局，預計 2027 年春季至秋季 120 美元以上價格帶產品將
擴大市佔率，但價位低於 120 美元 的產品可能因競爭激烈而表現較疲軟 。 
營收展望 調降。公司下調 FY27 營收展望至年增 4-5% ( 前次為 7-8%)，反
映 Hoka 和 UGG 貢獻分別下修至高、低個位數成長 ，而歐洲 物流延誤需求
將無法完全補上；不過， IEEPA 退稅 仍支撐 FY27 毛利率及營利率財測分別
上調至 58.5% 、23.0% 。短期而言，預計 3Q FY27 營收年增低個位數，遜
於市場預期之高個位數成長 。 未來將 保持庫存緊縮 策略， 並專注全價銷售。 
投資建議  
我們預估 Deckers FY26-28 獲利年複合成長率大幅趨緩至僅 4% (F Y20-26
為 24%) 將壓抑股價動能 ；維持「持有」評等，目標價下調 至 86.6 美元，
基於 FY27-28 平均每股盈餘預估 11 倍，對比歷史本益比區間為 10-35 倍。 
投資風險  
上檔風險為消費較預期強勁及品牌人氣提升。

### Page 2

美國 Deckers

十月 27, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

Deckers  10 月 23 日法說會重點摘要  
 2Q FY27 稅後淨 利年增 17%至 3.1 億 美 元，折合 每股盈餘 2.32 美 元，
優於市場預期 28%， 主因利潤率超前，及美國政府退稅貢獻 每股盈餘
0.28 美元。由於歐洲第三方物流  (3PL) 轉型導致 的運輸延誤 以及部分
電商業務疲軟， 本季 營收低於指引 ，年增 4%、季增 45%至 14.8 億
美元。排除一次性 退稅利 益，毛利率仍年增 1.2、季增 0.9 個百分點
至 57.4% ，主要得益自 全價銷售 及產品組合優化 ；再加上費用控管，
營利率 23.2% 優於市場共識之 20.3%。 
 市場 別營收：美國  (佔總營收 57%) 再度轉弱至年 減 0.4% ，主要受
運動鞋領域促銷活動增加之 影響 ，公司並未參與削價競爭 。國際市場 
(43%) 年增 9%，亞太地區為主要成長來源 ，而歐洲 第三方物流運輸
延誤 造成的負面影響約 2,200 萬美元。  
 品牌別營收： Hoka ( 佔總營收 46%) 年增 7%，批發業務與 DTC 銷售
分別年增 9% (升溫)、 4% (快速 冷卻 ，反映美國市場面臨壓力 )。UGG 
(53%) 年增 4%，批發業務年增 7%，抵銷 DTC 通路 8%衰退。其他
品牌  (1%) 則年減 54%。 
 產品別表現： 核心產品系列銷售 良好，其中以 Bondi 系列最為突出，
Clifton Pro 亦廣受好評。然而，消費者和批發合作夥伴的回饋表明，
今年有部分產品更新效果不如預 期，未來數季將進行一些改善工作。  
 通路別營收：批發業務  (佔總營收 74%) 年增加速至 6%，直接面對
消費者  (DTC ，26%) 則陷入年減 2%。 
 2Q FY27 庫存週轉天數 年增 6%、但季減 2%至 128 天， 仍低於過去
十年同期平均水準之 147 天。公司計畫保持庫存緊縮，並專注於全價
銷售。 
 管理層下調 FY27 營收展望至年增 4-5% (前次為 7-8%)；Hoka 和 UGG
貢獻分別下修至年增 高、低個 位 數。然而，考量 IEEPA 退稅挹注 ，FY27
毛利率及營利率財測則分別上調至 58.5% 、23.0%。加計 FY27 股票
回購 10 億美元，現估 稀釋後每股盈餘落在 7.80-7.95 美元。 
 若不計入 IEEPA 退稅挹注， 公司 預計 FY27 毛利率及營利率 分別約 為
57.0 %、略高於 21.5% ，大致符合市場預期。  
 短期而言，預計 3Q FY27 營收將 年增低個位數， 低於市場預期 ；品牌
別方面， Hoka 、UGG 將分別年增低中、 低個位數。 3Q FY27 稀釋後
每股盈餘預估落在 3.52-3.60 美元 (加計 IEEPA 退稅挹注 0.24 美元) 。 
 Mach 8 已於秋季初上市 ，為迄今為止 Mach 系列中表現最好的一款。
預計 2027 年 1 月推出 Bondi 10 和 Fly Pace，4 月 Mach Pro 接續亮相 。
6 月將 進一步 發售 Bondi Ultra X ，售價 325 美元/雙，其三密度 eTPU
中底提升緩震性能 至全新高度，標誌著 HOKA 創新技術的 重大躍進 。 
 UGG 銷售高峰集中在 11 月底至 12 月初。去年市場環境良好且 天氣
宜人 ，銷售季表現非常強勁，帶動 12 月及 1 月訂單追加； 預計今年
補貨量不會達到去年水準。 
 管理層觀察到消費者行為的 兩大轉變： (1) 消費者越來越追求折扣，

### Page 3

美國 Deckers

十月 27, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  3

客流量集中在促銷活動期間 ；及 (2) 消費者購買商品更 注重 目的性，
優先考慮 「即買即穿」 和具實用 性的產品 ，甚至等到接近實際需求時
才會購買 (延後購買 )。 
 美國市場感受到較大 壓力。目前消費者會盡可能透過線上批發商購買
產品 ，而這些批發商促銷力道非常 大。公司不願陷入這種過度促銷的
氛圍，因此美國 DTC 銷售成長將低於批發業務。  
 2027 年春季 至秋季，公司將持續 強化美國和歐洲批發分銷管道布局 ，
尤其價位在 120 美元以上的產品可望擴大市佔率， 但價位區間低於
120 美元的產品可能因競爭激烈 而流失市佔率 。 
 截至 2Q FY27 止，公司已回購股票 6.37 億美元，佔已發行 股數 5%，
遠超年初預期；而 剩餘授權額度約 44 億美元。

圖 6: 公司概況  圖 7: UGG 及 Hoka 為主力品牌 
成立於 1973 年， Deckers 為全球生活風格與專業運動鞋類、
服飾及配件領導品牌之一 ， 旗下品牌包括 UGG、Hoka、Teva、 
Koolaburra 和 AHNU 。公司產品銷往 50 多個國家，批發合作
夥伴貢獻 FY2 6 營收 59%，其餘 41%則來自直接面對消費者
銷售  (包括直營店及電商) 。美國為 FY26 營收主要來源地區，
佔比達 58%，但國際市場  (42%) 成長較快。

ESG 圖表定義  
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1 排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合
物(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1 排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合
物(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、
冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 = 8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

股價說明  等級  定義  
 增加持股  (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。  
 持有  (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。  
 降低持股  (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。  
 未評等  (NR)  凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。  
 受法規限制  
未評等  (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和 /或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交
流，其中包括提供評等給投資人參考。  
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價 -現價)/現價

前日收盤價                                   2 5 4 . 50元

洪士勛 
chris.hong@ctbcsis.com

封測  
力成(6239 TT)           法說 /座談摘要  
3Q26營運表現亮眼，營收與毛利率均創下歷史新高  
⚫ AI基礎建設投資強勁，持續支撐半導體及記憶體產業需求  
⚫ 力成與 Broadcom 於新加坡成立合資公司共同開發 CPO 
⚫ 處分毛利較低事業，改善成本結構  
結論：3Q26營運表現亮眼，單季合併營收達 253.34億元，毛利率 24.0%，
營收與毛利率均創下歷史新高，封裝與測試產能利用率均超過 90%，
4Q26將隨營收增加續揚。展望 4Q26營運，預期 DRAM與 NAND/eSSD
需求持續維持高檔，高階 FCBGA與邏輯封裝業務延續成長動能，獲利
可望進一步攀升；消費性電子因記憶體漲價拉貨持續保守。公司積極佈
局 FOPLP、CPO、3DIC及高階 FCBGA，先進封裝業務已佔集團營收
30%以上，故我們持續看好公司在先進封裝業務之成長表現。

一、3Q26財報概況：  
1.合併營收 :253.34 億元 (QoQ+9.6% ， YoY+26.9%) ，毛利率
24.0%(Q oQ+2.2ppts，YoY+7.9ppts)。 
2.營業利益 :44.72 億元 (QoQ+26.7%， YoY+117.9%)；營業利益率
17.7%(QoQ+2.4ppts，YoY+7.4ppts)。  
3.稅後淨利 :35.65億元(QoQ+25.1%，YoY+75.2%)，營業淨利率 14.1%。 
4.每股盈餘 :3.55元；每股淨值 75.04元。

二、3Q26營運概況：   
1.營收比重 (依服務類別 ): 封裝占 67%(QoQ+1ppts) 、測試占
24%(QoQ+1ppts)、SiP/Module 占 9% (QoQ -2ppts)。

2.營收比重 (依產品類別 ): Logic IC占 43%(QoQ+2ppts)，出貨量續增，子
公司超豐簽訂 LTA 帶動營收 QoQ、 YoY 續增雙位數； NAND 占
28%(QoQ -1ppts)，AI 伺服器帶旺  eSSD 需求，帶動營收持續成長；
DRAM 占 20%(QoQ+1ppts)，訂單能見度高，推升營收表現穩定增長；
SiP/Module 占 9%(QoQ -2ppts)。

3.CPO:與 Broadcom 於新加坡成立合資公司共同開發 CPO 與 Optical 
Engine，目前所有驗證均順利進行中，量產 計劃維持不變。

4.先進封裝 :積極投入 FOPLP、3DIC、TSV DRAM 及高階 FCBGA等技
術開發，目前先進封裝已佔集團營收 30%以上。

5.資本支出 :前三季累計的資本支出規模約 251億元，主要用於建置先進
封裝高階設備與基礎設施。此外針對  4Q26 的資金需求（含 Broadcom
投資專案），公司於 3Q26 季末已完成後續資金準備。

### Page 2

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /10 /28

2

三、營運展望：  
1.記憶體 :AI及雲端基礎建設投資維持強勁，持續支撐半導體及記憶體產業需求。 DRAM及
NAND Flash 需求仍大於供給，產品價格趨勢有利於記憶體產業營收成長。   
2.邏輯 IC:隨著 AI市場需求持續增溫，公司在高階 FCBGA同步受惠，既有高階產品需求亦
持續擴大。預期 4Q26高階封裝業務將延續成長動能，維持穩健向上的發展趨勢。受惠於市
場供需持續緊俏，多數客戶已接受價格調整。隨著原物料價格上升所帶動的價格反映，預期
4Q26將進一步挹注營收及獲利成長，並對整體獲利表現帶來正面貢獻。  
3.先進封測 :FOPLP部分，客戶端的產品驗證正持續推進，並朝 2027年量產目標穩定發展。
此外，隨著市場需求及量產信心度提升，客戶在新產品導入的需求亦顯著增加，對未來業務
注入新的成長動能。  
4.預估 4Q26營收與毛利率將在 3Q26基礎上持續增長。目前封裝與測試產能利用率均超過
90%，4Q26稼動率將維持高檔，公司將透過廠房重新佈局及汰舊換新提升生產效率。

四、公司 /產業看法：  
1.矽光子與 CPO前景:AI浪潮推動資料傳輸與高效能運算需求， CPO與 Optical Engine 成為
未來關鍵技術。公司從傳統記憶體封裝廠轉型為 AI時代的異質整合與光學封裝合作夥伴，
以高毛利、高技術門檻解決方案提升附加價值。   
2.產能佈局與資本支出規劃 :封裝與測試產能利用率均已超過 90%。2025~2026年持續投入先
進封裝高階設備，精簡中國大陸傳統封裝廠等低毛利海外資產，資金與研發能量聚焦於台灣
高階先進製程據點。   
3.價格調漲與成本結構改善 :受原物料與封裝材料成本上升影響，公司適度將成本反映給客
戶，客戶接受度高，並依市場行情進行個位數至雙位數百分比不等的價格調整。 4Q26在產
能稼動率維持高檔及價格效益的雙重帶動下，預期獲利將進一步攀升。   
4.業務獲利結構與轉型時程 :營收結構將從過去高度依賴標準型 DRAM與 NAND封裝，轉變
為由記憶體封測、傳統封測與先進封測共同驅動。先進封裝 (包含 FCBGA、FOPLP、Bumping、
3D IC等)目前已佔集團營收 30%以上，預期2027年起 FOPLP與 CPO等新產品商業化量產，
帶動營收規模與獲利占比持續顯著提升。

五、Q&A： 
Q:公司如何看待未來幾年 FOPLP與 CoPoS在高階封裝市場的競合關係？與台積電合作之可
能性及同業競爭實力？   
A:台積電採用的 CoPoS與力成的 FOPLP為相同製程，差別在於面板尺寸。力成採用 510mm 
x515mm面板，大於台積電的 310mmx310mm，因此成本具優勢，但技術門檻更高。公司不
對同業競爭實力做評論，經營理念是直接服務半導體產品或系統模組公司並參與設計開發，
提升附加價值，但也期待有機會與晶圓廠建立供應鏈後段策略聯盟。

### Page 3

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /10 /28

3 
Q:目前先進封裝業務占集團營收比重及未來成長動能？   
A:若將 FCBGA、FOPLP、Bumping、3D等先進封裝相關業務納入計算，目前已占集團營收
30%以上。隨著 FOPLP等新產品逐漸明確並進入量產， 2027年起公司成長將不再依賴傳統
記憶體景氣循環，而是由記憶體封測、傳統封測與先進封測共同驅動，先進封裝營收規模及
佔比將持續提升。  
Q:力成目前的技術是否能夠做到 EMIB / bridge 的封裝方式？目前是否已經有客戶採用此封
裝技術？  
A:公司目前已具備 EMIB-like的技術能力， Intel是將 Bridge Die 嵌入在 ABF載板內，而公
司是將 Bridge Die 嵌入 polymer。EMIB需要 Mix Bridge Bump ，做不同 Bump-pitch在上面
會影響產能，惟公司目前仍以內部產能為主，暫未打算向外提供服務。  
Q:公司設備交期是否會影響 FOPLP的量產時程？   
A:半導體設備市場需求雖仍吃緊，但在採購、工程人員與策略供應商全力配合協助下，缺機
台情況已明顯改善。力成 2027年至 2028年的產能擴充均按計劃進行，不會因機器設備影響
未來的 FOPLP量產時程。  
Q:當初選擇 510mm x 515mm 面板尺寸的主要考量？   
A:公司在 2014~2015 年即針對各種 IC尺寸進行數據化模擬計算，結果顯示 510mmx515mm
的面板面積利用率優於 310mmx310mm 或 600mmx600mm；且在製程化學與品質控制上，從
中心點到邊緣的品質均一性技術瓶頸較為可控，因此採用 510mmx515mm 尺寸。

[台湾]～生活必需品を中心にインフレ加速も、輸出・入額ともに月次ベースで過去最高を更新している～ 
７日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.73％となり、前月（同＋2.04％）から加速
して 2024 年３月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比も＋0.44％と前月（同▲0.01％）から上昇に転
じており、 原油価格の再上昇を受けてエネルギー価格が上昇しているほか、 天候不順の影響により
生鮮品をはじめとする食料品価格も上昇するなど、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強まってい
る。 なお、 野菜 ・ 果物とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年同月比＋2.52％となり、 前月 （同
＋2.30％）から加速して７ヵ月ぶりの高い伸びとな った。前月比も＋0.06％と前月（同▲0.15％）
から２ヵ月ぶりの上昇に転じており、 エネルギー価格の上昇による輸送コストの押し上げを反映し
て財価格に上昇圧力がかかる一方、 景気の先行き不透明感を反映してサービス物価は下落基調にあ
るなど、物価の動きにばらつきが生じている。 
８日に発表された９月の輸出額は前年同月比＋60.9％となり、前月（同＋41.0％）から伸びが加
速した。 前月比も＋7.5％と前月 （同＋10.4％） に続いて２ヵ月連続で拡大しており、 持ち直しの動
きを強めるとともに、単月ベースの輸出額は過去最高を更新した。財別では、主力の輸出財である
半導体など電子部品関連や電気機械関連で拡大が続いているほか、 化学製品関連も堅調な動きをみ
せるとともに、商品市況の再上昇を反映して鉱物資源関連の輸出額も押し上げられた。国・地域別
では、中国本土向けが頭打ちとなり、日本向けも下振れする一方、米国向けや欧州向けが堅調に推
移しているほか、ASEAN などアジア新興国向けにも旺盛な動きがみられる。一方、輸入額は前年同

### Page 2

2/4 
月比＋51.7％となり、 前月 （同＋44.3％） から伸びが加速した。 前月比も＋7.7％と前月 （同＋6.4％）
に続いて２ヵ月連続で拡大しており、 持ち直しの動きを強めるとともに、 輸出額と同様に単月ベー
スの輸入額も過去最高を更新した。 財別では、 輸出の堅調さを反映して機械製品関連の輸入が堅調
な動きをみせるとともに、プラスチック関連など素材・部材関連の輸入も底堅いほか、金をはじめ
とする貴金属関連の輸 入が大幅に拡大したことも輸入額を押し上げた。その結果、貿易収支は＋
236.30 億ドルと前月 （＋222.98 億ドル） から黒字幅が拡大して、 月次ベースで過去最高となった。

[タイ]～エネルギー物価の上昇がインフレ圧力を強める一方、住宅価格やサービス物価に不透明感～ 
６日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.82％となり、 中銀の目標レンジ （２±１％）
の範囲内で推移しているものの、 前月 （同＋2.53％） から加速して４ヵ月ぶりの高い伸びとなった。
ただし、前月比は＋0.25％と前月（同＋0.56％）から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、原油価格の再上
昇を反映してエネルギー価格の上昇が続く一方、 穀物や生鮮品など食料品価格の上昇ペースは鈍化
するなど、 生活必需品の物価動向にはばらつきがみられる。食料品とエネルギーを除いたコアイン
フレ率は前年同月比＋1.50％となり、引き続き目標レンジ内で推移しているものの、前月（同＋
1.44％） から加速して2023 年５月以来の高い伸びとなった。 前月比は＋0.01％と前月 （同＋0.20％）
から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、 エネルギー価格の上昇が輸送コストを押し上げるとともに、 バー
ツ安に伴う輸入物価の上昇も重なり、幅広い財価格に上昇圧力がかかっている。一方、住宅価格が
下振れしたほか、 景気の先行き不透明感の高まりがサービス物価の重しとなるなど、 物価を巡る動
きにはばらつきが生じている。

[フィリピン]～生活必需品のみならず、幅広くインフレ圧力が強まり、インフレ率は再び加速している～ 
６日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋7.2％となり、６ヵ月連続で中銀の目標レン
ジ （２～４％） を上回る推移が続くとともに、 前月 （同＋6.1％） から加速して５ヵ月ぶりの高い伸
びとなった。前月比も＋0.95％と前月（同＋0.59％）から上昇ペースが加速しており、原油価格の
再上昇を反映してエネルギー価格が上昇しているうえ、 穀物や生鮮品など食料品価格も上昇するな
ど、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強まったことが影響している。食料品とエネルギーを除い
たコアインフレ率も前年同月比＋4.7％となり、５ヵ月連続で目標レンジを上回るとともに、前月
（同＋4.1％）から加速して 2023 年 11 月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比も＋0.59％と前月（同
＋0.37％） から上昇ペースが加速しており、 エネルギー価格の上昇を受けた輸送コストの増加に加
え、通貨ペソ安に伴う輸入物価の押し上げも重なり、財・サービスを問わず幅広く価格上昇圧力が
強まる動きがみられる。

[マレーシア]～半導体など電子部品関連の生産は引き続き旺盛だが、分野ごとのばらつきが鮮明に～ 
９日に発表された８月の鉱工業生産は前年同月比＋5.0％となり、前月（同＋4.7％）から伸びが
加速した。前月比も＋0.54％と前月（同▲0.10％）から４ヵ月ぶりの増加に転じており、これまで
の頭打ち傾向に変化の兆しがうかがえる。 分野別では、 原油や天然ガスをはじめとする鉱業部門の
生産が３ヵ月ぶりの増加に転じるなど持ち直しの動きをみせる一方、 製造業の生産は３ヵ月ぶりの
減少に転じるなど対照的な動きとなっている。 経済活動の動向に連動する傾向がある発電量は３ヵ
月連続で増加しており、 全体としては持ち直しの動きが続いている様子がうかがえる。 製造業のう
ち、 主力の輸出財である半導体など電子部品関連や電気機器関連の生産は旺盛な動きをみせる一方、
自動車など輸送用機械関連、 石油化学製品関連、 木製品や縫製品など軽工業関連の生産は力強さを
欠いており、分野ごとのばらつきが鮮明になっている。

維持 評等  
買進 (Buy)  
VRU 預 計 部 分 採 用 ZQD ， 帶 動 單 機 價 值 量 再 提 升 
⚫ 3Q26 營收略低預期，惟產品組合優化推升毛利 率 
⚫ VRU 接頭升級 ZQD ，單機價值量提升 69% 
⚫ QD 產能逐步開出，預期明年深振與越南廠續擴產  
3Q26 營收略低預期，惟產品組合優化推升毛利率 ： 富世達 9 月營收
達 16.13 億元，月增 5.5% 、年增 32.17% ；惟 3Q26 營收低於我們先前
預估約 8%，主 要因手機 客戶拉 貨動 能不如 預期 。原先 預期 手機相 關
訂單將逐月增溫，惟受新機發表前後拉貨節奏調整影響所致。不過
GB300 與 VR200 QD 於 3Q26 持續出貨，伺服器業務動能強勁，有助
於改善產品組合及提升獲利能力。我們預期伺服器相關營收占比將由
2Q26 的 56% 提升至 80～85%，因此上調 3Q26 毛利率預估至 37.05 %，
EPS 亦調升至 16.97 元。  
VRU 接頭升級 ZQD， 單機價值量提升 69%：NVIDIA 下一代平台 Rubin 
Ultra (VRU) 平台因應更高的冷卻液流量需求，部分原採用 MQD/UQD
的接頭將升級為 ZQD，以提升接頭流量能力，並降低高流量運作下的
壓降與洩漏風險。依目前設計， Compute Tray 上方 4 組接頭將改採
ZQD ，且所有浮動式接頭 均將改採 ZQD ，Rack 端的 CDM QD 亦將全
面升級為 ZQD 。儘管 QD 總使用量維持不變，但 ZQD 的單價較
MQD/UQD 翻倍。受惠於 QD 規格升級與單價提升，我們預估 VRU 世
代 QD 產值將較 VR200 世代增加約 69%， 有望進一步挹注富世達的營
收與獲利成長。  
QD 產能逐步開出，預期明年深振與越南廠續擴產 ：QD 目前既有月產
能約 300 萬顆，稼動率已達滿載；隨新產能於 10 月開出，月產能將
提升至 450 萬顆，目前新產能稼動率約 60% ～70% 。考量既有產能已
滿載，公司規劃明年持續擴產，深圳及越南廠均納入擴產規劃，惟實
際新增產能仍待確認，預計資本支出約  3～5 億元，以支應後續訂單
成長需求。  
我們本次 主要 上修 2027 年獲利 9.8%，並維持買進建議，目標價 3,230
元(2027 年 EPS X 26PER) 。 
風險因子 : 伺服器 平台轉換不順 、AI 需求不振。

 伺服器業務優於預期， 4Q26 營運續強 : 3Q26 營收 45.02 億元，季增 20.4% 、年增 26.2%， 較我們原先預估低約 8%， 主要受到手機客戶拉貨動能不如預期影響。
惟 GB300 與 VR200 QD 持續出貨，帶動伺 服器業務維持強勁成長，預期伺服器
相關營收占比將由 2Q26 的 56% 提升至 80～85% ，有助於優化產品組合並提升
獲利能力。因此我們上調 3Q26 毛利率預估至 37.05 %，EPS 亦調升至 16.97 元。 
展望 4Q26 ，隨 VR200 出貨量進一步增加、多項 ASIC 平台 QD 逐步放量， 我們
預期營收將季增 18.7%至 53. 44 億元；受惠伺服器產品占比提升及生產效率改
善，毛利率可望再季增 約 0.3 個百分點至 37. 35%，EPS 預估為 20. 22 元，營收
與獲利均有望續創成長。  
雙面水冷板推動接頭設計變革，富世達單機價值量可望再提升 :目前 AMD 與
AWS 已採用雙面水冷板設計，根據供應鏈調查， Google TPU V9 及 NVIDIA 
VRU 亦有望跟進。隨著垂直供電 (VPD) 設計導入，伺服器內部可供水路配置的
空 間受 到壓縮 ，帶 動雙面 水冷 板及微 型快 速接頭 (MQD/Mini QD) 的 應用 需求 。
另一方面，為滿足更高冷卻液流量需求，部分接頭亦有望由 UQD 升級至單價較
高的 ZQD 。上述設計變化若逐步落地，將分別透過接頭用量增加、微型接頭導
入及高單價產品升級，創造單機 QD 價值量的額外成長空間，進一步挹注富世
達 2027 年營收與獲利表現。

Document 695: 時事雑感(2026年11月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/時事雑感(2026年11月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

注目のキーワード 「金融レジリエンス」
資産形成というと、 NISAや株式投資などで 「お金を増やす」 ことに目が向きがちです。 一方、 物価上昇や失業、 病気、 災害
など、 家計には予期せぬ出来事が生じます。 特にたくわえが十分でない世帯では、 長期的な投資に先立ち、 急な出費や収
入減に対応できる生活防衛資金の確保も重要です。 こう した備えは、 投資した資産を不利な時期に取り崩すことを避け、 長
期的な資産形成を続けるための支えにもなります。 したがって資産形成には、 「どれだけ資産を増やせるか」 だけでなく、
「家計が予期せぬシ ョ ッ クにどれだけ耐え、 そこから立て直せるか」 という視点も欠かせません。 家計がシ ョ ッ クに耐える力
の重要性は、 近年のコロナ禍や物価上昇を経て、 改めて認識されています。
こう した視点に関わる概念の一つが 「金融レジリエンス」 です。 金融レジリエンスとは、 経済的なシ ョ ッ クに直面しても生
活を維持し、 立て直す力を指します。 OECD/INFEの国際調査では、 「主な収入源を失った場合に、 借入れや転居をせずに

生活費をどのく らいの期間まかなえるか」 「月収に相当する大きな支出を、 借入れや家族 ・ 友人からの援助なしに支払える
か」 といった設問で測定しています。
もっ とも、 生活を立て直すために必要なのは、 金融面での備えや対処力だけではありません。 困難な状況を受け止め、 適
応し、 立ち直っていく心理的な力、 すなわち 「心理的レジリエンス」 も重要です。
また、 どれだけ備え、 計画を立てていても、 将来には予測できない部分が残ります。 収入や物価、 金利、 投資収益、 家族状
況は不確実で、 資産形成に絶対安心の 「唯一の正解」 はありません。 そこで参考になるのが 「ネガティブ ・ ケイパビリティ」 で
す。 これは、 すぐには答えの出ない状況や、 不確実性、 曖昧さに直面しても、 性急に結論を求めず、 その状況に耐えながら考
え続ける力を意味します。
経済的なシ ョ ッ クに備え、 対処し、 生活を立て直す 「金融レジリエンス」 。 困難な状況に心理的に適応し、 回復する 「心理的

レジリエンス」 。 そして、 不確実性と付き合いながら考え続ける 「ネガティブ ・ ケイパビリティ」 。 これら三つの力をあわせて育
むことが、 不確実性の高い時代における資産形成を支える重要な視点ではないでし ょ うか。
（資産運用調査部　研究員　小林 菜）
時の政府にと っては鬼門とされる米国の中間選挙が11月3日に行われる。 1934年以降の23回の中間選挙を
振り返ると、 現職大統領の政党が上下両院とも議席を増やしたのは1934年のF.ルーズベル ト大統領と、 2002年
のG.W.ブッシュ大統領の2回しかない。 ルーズベル ト大統領は大恐慌下のニューディール政策、 ブッシュ大統領は
9.11同時テロへの対応がそれぞれ支持された。 因みに、 その23回の選挙のうち、 下院の議席数を増やしたのは前
述の2名のほかには、 1998年のクリン トン大統領だけである （同選挙では上院の議席数は変わらなかった） 。 一方、
上院については前述の2名の他に、 1962年のケネディ、 1970年のニクソン、 2018年の トランプ、 2022年のバイ

デン各大統領が議席を増やしている。 下院に比べ、 上院で政権が議席を増やしているケースが多い背景には、 1回
の選挙における上院の改選議席数は全体の3分の1であることが影響している。
さて、 こう した過去のケースを振り返ると、 中間選挙では余程の実績や追い風が無いと、 下院議席数を減らす結
果となる厳しい 「中間テス ト」 ということがわかる。 現 トランプ政権は物価高やイラン問題で国内の批判も高いこと
から、 過去の例に倣えば下院共和党の議席数は減少する可能性が高い。 現在の議席数は、 下院 （全435議席） では
共和党は218、 民主党は214議席 （無所属共和党員1、 空席2） と、 両党の議席数は拮抗し、 逆転の可能性は十分に
ある。 これに対し上院 （全100議席） では、 共和党は53議席を有しているが、 そのうち22議席が改選対象と、 守るべ
き議席数が多い。
米国の各種世論調査では、 下院のみならず、 上院でも民主党が過半数を奪還するとの見方もある。 上下両院で
民主党が過半数を握る事態となれば、 向こう2年間の トランプ政権は予算案を中心に厳しい政策運営を迫られ、 債

券市場や株式市場への悪影響も危惧される。   （嶌峰 義清）
時事雑感
10第一ライフ研レポー ト 2026.11

Document 698: 月度中国宏观洞察：三季度经济增速超预期，内需修复仍待观察

Source PDF: all report pdf/月度中国宏观洞察：三季度经济增速超预期，内需修复仍待观察.pdf
Report source: 浦银国际证券
Date: 2026-10-27

### Page 1

浦银国际研究

政策展望：政策重心转向存量政策落地，全年增长目标实现基本无虞。
我们预计今年四季度经济增速或小幅回升至 4.6%，全年增速约 4.6%。
我们认为今年接下来的政策重点将落在现有政策的执行上， 新增政策刺
激的可能性明显下降。财政政策上，1.3 万亿元超长期特别国债已于 10
月 16 日发行完毕；截至 10 月 25 日，地方政府新增专项债发行进度约
84%； 财政部启用5,500 亿元地方政府债务结存限额， 但地方支出偏慢、
土地出让收入下滑仍制约稳投资的进展。8,000 亿元新型政策性金融工
具已投放超 2,500 亿元，后续效果取决于配套融资和开工进度。今年接
下来财政政策重点或放在现有政策的执行和成效观望上。 地方政府专项
债和新型政策性金融工具均还有一定发行额度。货币政策上，考虑到房
贷贴息政策落地和 PSL 贷款定向降息，我们认为货币政策将以流动性呵
护和结构性工具为主，不再预期今年降准或降息。房地产政策上，8月
以来中央和地方政策的特点是需求端刺激和房地产 销售制度改革同时
推进。我们认为这有助于稳定市场预期，短期最有可能看到的是部分城
市成交改善， 但未必能够快速扭转下行趋势， 中期则需要观察房企融资、
项目交付和新开工能否逐步恢复。

汇率展望：维持人民币稳中略升判断，预计年底美元兑人民币汇率在
6.65 附近。9 月以来人民币持续走强，不论是兑美元还是一篮子货币的
CFETS 指数均走强。出口增长、贸易顺差扩大和企业结汇形成的外汇供
给是主要支撑，中美关系缓和亦支撑汇市信心。接下来，贸易结汇和增
长预期仍提供支撑，但美国通胀及利率路径仍是主要约束。若美联储加
息预期升温或摩擦升级，人民币汇率可能阶段性回吐涨幅。

投资风险：下行风险：内需和房地产修复弱于预期，输入性通胀推高利
率预期、AI 投资降温或中美贸易科技摩擦升级，拖累外需和风险偏好。
上行风险：政策落地及民间投资改善超预期，美国对华关税降幅大于我
们的预期。

月度中国宏观洞察：三季度经济增速超
预期，内需修复仍待观察 
数据回顾：三季度经济增速超预期， 但内需疲软态势仍未
根本扭转 
三季度经济增速超预期回升，名义增速续创新高。中国三季度实际 GDP 同
比增速回升 0.2 个百分点至 4.5%，高于市场预期的 4.4%，结束了二季度的
放缓趋势。名义GDP 同比增速小幅上升 0.1 个百分点至 6.0%，为近18 个季
度最高（图表 1）， 继续受益于中东冲突推升的原油价格以及AI产业链涨价。
分行业来看， 三季度第二产业同比增速回升0.4 个百分点至 3.4%， 成为拉动
整体增速回升的主要因素，其中油价阶段性回落带动石化链生产修复、 投资
企稳使建筑业生产未再明显走弱是主要原因 。第三产业同比增速小幅上升
0.1 个百分点至 5.2%。AI相关科技服务需求延续高景气（前三季度信息传输
软件和信息技术服务业、 租赁和商务服务业增加值分别增长10.4%、12.3%），
叠加居民出行改善带动服务消费修复， 支撑第三产业， 但内需疲软仍制约服
务业的更大弹性。 按支出法来看，净出口对经济增速的拉动较二季度上升 0.9
个百分点至 1.8%。消费对经济增速的贡献上升 0.2 个百分点到 2.2%，投资
（资本形成）的贡献下降约 0.9 个百分点至 0.6%（图表 2）。此外，前三季
度居民人均实际可支配收入累计增速相比上半年上升 0.2 个百分点至 4.4%，
为四季度消费提供一定支撑。

9 月经济数据边际改善， 供给端延续修复， 但内需的疲软态势仍未根本扭转。
在需求端，9 月社会消费品零售总额同比增速比 8 月加快 0.7 个百分点至
1.1%、好于预期，主要受中秋假期错位、油价上涨和低基数推动。剔除汽车
后的社会消费品零售总额同比增长 3.9%，汽车零售下降 21.2%仍是最大拖
累（图表 3）。固定资产投资累计同比跌幅持平于 7.2%，结束此前的连续回
落。 这主要得益于制造业累计同比跌幅的收窄，但基建投资和房地产投资跌
图表 1：三季度实际 GDP 同比增速超预期回升，

 图表 2：按支出法来看，净出口贡献的改善是主要
推动力，投资贡献显著下滑

升至 5.5%，同时出口同比增速再次超预期，加快到 36.3%，得益于持续强劲
的全球 AI 投资（图表 8）。我们预计短期内 AI 投资周期仍将对出口形成支
撑，但随着前期“抢出口”效应消退、高基数效应显现，四季度出口增速或
呈高位缓步回落态势。

收窄，但基建投资和房地产投资跌幅仍在扩大

政策展望：定向增量政策刺激如期推出， 政策重点将在落
实现有政策和观望成效上 
9 月起，增量政策刺激如期而至。在货币政策方面，央行在 9 月 29 日宣布
下调 PSL 利率 25 个基点，支持范围扩至“六张网” ，并新增7000 亿元再贷
款额度。在财政政策方面，财政部在 10 月宣布启用 5500 亿元地方政府债
务结存限额，支持地方财政和项目建设。值得注意的是去年也有 5000 亿元
结存限额的使用，今年仅增加了 500 亿元用于专项债支持。 但正如我们此前
预期的，政策刺激以定向支持为主，幅度较为适度。在财政和货币政策协同
发力、低基数效应的影响下，我们预计今年四季度经济增速或小幅回升 0.1
个百分点到 4.6%。 全年经济增速或落在4.6%， 实现全年4.5%-5.0%区间内的
经济增速目标基本无虞。 基于此， 我们认为今年接下来的政策重点将落在现
有政策的执行上，新增政策刺激的可能性明显下降。

（一） 财政政策

在财政政策上， 加快资金使用并形成实物工作量仍是核心任务。根据我们对
政府债的跟踪，截至 10 月 25 日，地方政府新增专项债累计发行 3.71 万亿
元， 年内剩余额度约6,880 亿元，发行进度约 84%。 其中，9 月单月发行 6,204
亿元、同比增长约 50%，10 月发行有所减慢（图表 16）。1.3 万亿元超长期
特别国债已于 10 月 16 日发行完毕。不过，融资进度的改善尚未充分转化
为财政支出和实际需求。财政部 10 月 23 日公布的数据显示，前三季度全
国一般公共预算支出同比增长 1.0%，其中地方支出仅增长 0.2%；全国政府
性基金预算支出同比下降 16.2%，土地出让收入下降 27.9%。收入端则明显
好于支出端，前三季度一般公共预算收入同比增长 5.1%，我们自行计算的
广义赤字率在 9 月继续下滑（图表 15）。这表明，地方财力承压与资金使
用偏慢仍对稳投资形成制约。

投资端最明确的进展，是 8,000 亿元新型政策性金融工具进入加快投放阶
段，并更加重视带动民间资本参与。截至 9 月末，三家政策性银行合计投放
已超过 1,000 亿元，支持项目约 400 个，涉及项目总投资约 2.1 万亿元（图
表 17）。投向上更加侧重人工智能、数字经济、先进制造、绿色低碳及民
间投资项目（图表 18）。与此同时，国家发展改革委 10 月 10 日表示将加
快推进“十五五”规划和“六张网”重大项目开工，10 月 21 日进一步提出
加强新型政策性金融工具对民间投资项目的支持、常态化向民营企业推介
项目。我们认为四季度投资的政策支撑将更多体现为政府资金补充资本金、
银行配套贷款和民间资本共同参与。剩余约 5,500 亿元额度能否在年内形成
实物工作量，取决于项目收益机制、融资落实及开工条件能否同步改善
（1,500 亿元额度在今年一季度已经提前下达）。

我们预计今年接下来政策重点或仍在现有政策的执行和成效观望 上。目前
地方政府专项债和新型政策性金融工具均还有一定额度可以发放，再推出
增量财政刺激的可能性比较低。

图表 15：我们计算的广义赤字率 9月继续下行  图表 16：地方政府专项债发行在 8-9月显著加速，
10月随着剩余额度减少而发行减速

（二）货币政策

近期货币政策的重点，已由单纯加大逆周期调节，转向“结构性宽松＋框架
改革”双线推进。9 月 29 日央行调整完善多项工具：将一年期 PSL 利率下
调 25 个基点至 1.50%，并把“六张网”建设纳入支持领域；科技创新和技
术改造再贷款增加 2,000 亿元至 1.4万亿元、 支持比例由60%提高至 100%；
支农支小再贷款和再贴现增加 5,000 亿元至 4.85 万亿元，其中民营企业再
贷款增加 3,000 亿元至 1.3 万亿元（参见：央行“四箭齐发” ，房贷贴息落地：
新一轮稳增长政策“组合拳”解读）。10月 19日，中国人民银行行长潘功胜
在金融街论坛宣布即将推出五项举措，包括设立应对非银机构大额赎回的
流动性支持工具、扩大常备借贷便利（SLF）适用机构范围、推动银行间与
交易所债券市场互联互通、便利境外机构在境内外汇市场开展人民币购售
等，并明确货币政策框架改革的三个方向： 择机将公开市场主要操作品种切
换为隔夜、 推动贷款定价基准由单一LPR转向多元、 逐步退出存贷款基准利
率。我们认为，“六张网”纳入PSL 支持范围进一步增强了货币政策配合财
政稳投资的导向，而政策利率“换锚”与贷款定价“双锚”改革着眼于提升
传导效率， 短期并不必然带来广谱利率下行， 后续结构性工具的支持范围、
资金供给及配套信贷仍值得重点关注。

我们预计今年接下来央行将以流动性呵护和结构性工具为主， 年内降准降
息的可能性显著降低。我们之前的预期是如果 9 月至四季度内需修复仍偏
弱、外需从高位明显回落，那么央行在今年年内仍有可能降息 10 个基点和
/或降准 25-50 个基点。但从目前的情况来看，这些假设情况并不会全部成
立， 尤其是在目前看来今年经济增速目标的实现基本无虞的情况下。再加上
我们期待已久的房地产贴息政策落地，央行也对抵押补充贷款进行了定向
降息，我们现在认为今年接下来时间央行不会再降准降息，货币政策仍以结
构性支持为主。降准或被延迟到明年一季度，而降息制约仍较多，如输入性
通胀压力、当前仍处在低位的银行净息差和短端市场利率（图表 20）。

（三）房地产政策 
为了支持房地产市场，8 月以来中央和地方陆续推出了一系列新政策。与此
前主要依靠放松限购、 降低首付不同， 这轮政策的特点是需求端刺激和房地
产销售制度改革同时推进（图表 21）。中央层面，在供应端，8 月底推出了
新房销售制度改革。 政策要求新项目在主体结构封顶后才能预售， 鼓励现房
销售， 并强化项目资金封闭管理和交付保障。 这有助于降低购房者对烂尾风
险的担忧， 但也意味着开发商的销售回款时点后移， 短期可能增加房企资金
压力。为此，中央同步发布了政策进一步拓宽房企融资渠道，包括支持符合
条件的房企再融资、 并购、 资产证券化， 以及租赁住房和城市更新REITs等。
然而，我们认为这一举措并不足以弥补现房销售对房企的流动性压力， 支持
力度或有待进一步加强。

在需求端，9 月底公布了首套房贷贴息政策，对符合条件的新发放商业房贷，
财政给予 1 个百分点贴息，最长 5 年，贴息本金上限 100 万元。房贷贴息
有望释放部分刚需购房需求，但对消费的提振相对有限。以 3.1%的房贷利
率为例，贴息额度内实际付息利率可降至 2.1%，接近 6 月 50 城 2.28%的毛
租金回报率，因此我们认为该政策对低线城市及低总价房源的刚需购房者
更具吸引力。不过，100万元贷款首期月供仅减少约 833 元，首付和还本仍
可能挤占家庭现金流。 我们估算， 一年试行期新增贷款对应的五年累计财政
贴息约 662 亿元，其中中央财政承担90%，整体财政负担相对可控。地方层
面，北京、上海、成都等城市也通过放宽购房资格、降低首付比例、提高公
积金贷款额度和提供购房补贴支持需求。

展望未来， 我们认为这轮政策有助于稳定市场预期， 但未必能够快速扭转房
地产下行趋势。短期最有可能看到的是部分城市成交改善； 中期则需要观察
房企融资、 项目交付和新开工能否逐步恢复。 房地产是否真正走出调整周期，
仍然取决于居民收入预期、房价预期和库存去化情况。  
图表 19： M2、社融和贷款存量增速继续下滑，
信贷需求仍弱 
 图表 20：短期市场利率仍维持在较低水平上

图表 21：今年 8月以来房地产政策一览  
时间 政策/ 城市 政策具体内容 
中央政府 
8 月 18日 公积金 9/20起拓宽公积金提取至装修、物业费，取消租房提取的收入比例门槛，支持灵活就业人员自愿缴存。 
8 月 28日 制度 新项目封顶后预售、优先现房销售，预售按揭竣工备案后发放。主办银行制与项目资金封闭管理，房贷期限上限延至 40年。 
8 月 28日 融资 支持房企再融资、并购及债券／ABS融资。拓展租赁住房与城市更新 REITs，融资更侧重项目。 
9 月 29日 贴息 10 月 1 日起首套新发商贷贴息 1 个百分点，最长 5 年、本金≤100 万元。房屋须≤120 ㎡且总价≤150万元，含新房及二手房。 
地方政府 
8 月 8日 北京 非京籍购房社保／个税统一降至1年。双缴存家庭首套公积金最高240万元，符合条件可至340万元。 
8 月 21日 上海 外环外二套首付降至 15%，以旧换新贷款补贴最高 5万元，符合“内换外”条件另补 3万元。推行房票与二手房收购作保租房。 
8 月 25日 成都 新房公积金首付 15%，补贴实际利息 20%、为期 1年、上限 2.5 万元。支持“拆旧建好”和存量盘活。 
9月 24日至今 多城 京沪广深等地落实销售新规，宁波 10 月 16 日跟进。旧项目分类过渡，土地款分期及分栋验收等配套缓释资金压力。

汇率展望 
9 月以来人民币保持偏强走势，10月进一步升值。10 月截止到 26 日，人民
币兑美元汇率在 10 月升值 0.55%，10 月 26 日 6.6681 的在岸人民币兑美元
即期汇率收盘价创下 2022 年 7 月以来的收盘新高。人民币对一篮子货币在
10 月稳中略微升值（图表 22）。较大规模的贸易顺差与实际结汇形成的外
汇供给，仍是人民币走强的重要支撑。9 月中国以美元计价的出口同比增长
36.3%，较8 月进一步加快；贸易顺差扩大至1,335.3 亿美元，为外汇市场提
供了较充裕的潜在供给（图表 24）。与此同时，外汇局公布的数据显示，9
月银行结汇 2,942 亿美元、售汇 2,380 亿美元，据此计算结售汇顺差为 562
亿美元，表明外汇兑换环节整体仍对人民币形成支持（图表 25）。此外，9
月以来持续的中美高层沟通有助于管理经贸风险，但不宜将关系改善视为
持续、单向的升值动力。

在政策意愿上，我们认为央行在允许市场供求影响汇率的同时， 仍会关注单
边预期与过快波动。一方面，以央行的中间价报价和彭博社调查的差值来模
拟央行对汇市的干预情况， 我们注意到在此次人民币兑美元汇率突破6.7 的
关键点位后，央行对中间价的干预并未增强（图表 23）。另一方面，10 月
15 日，潘功胜表示，全球失衡不能依靠汇率调节，汇率与经常账户之间不
存在简单线性关系。

展望年底， 我们继续维持人民币兑美元稳中略升、 美元兑人民币汇率在6.65
附近的基准判断。从内部看，较大的贸易顺差及结汇需求继续提供支撑，经
济增速回升也有助于稳定增长预期。从外部看， 美国通胀和利率路径仍是人
民币进一步升值的主要约束。在我们的基本假设——贸易收入继续转化为结
注：中央政策的时间是发布时间，地方政策的时间是施行时间
